Our vision is more along the lines of an interactive system, in which
recurrences are extracted and “easy” ones solved, but allowing the user to
step in to provide assertions (hopefully proved!) about the solutions to
difficult ones. Familiarity with recurrence extraction as a cost analysis
technique would hopefully lower the entry barrier of such a tool. We could
also hope that that same familiarity would enable users to
work backward from an unexpectedly poor recurrence to the code from
which it results (cf.
Benzinger 2004
).

Wang and Hoffmann 2019

adapt AARA to provide worst-case inputs that validate the tightness of the
produced bounds, which could be used to similar effect. Another direction
such a project could take would be to pull either the syntactic or the
semantic information back as additional interface-level components of a
language library, so as to modularize cost reasoning and take advantage of the
compositionality of our approach. However, this is not so straightforward.
One issue that arises is that the denotation a type that is appropriate for
analyzing an algorithm is not necessarily the one that is appropriate for
using it. For example, the recurrence extraction approach works best to
analyze binary search tree algorithms in terms of their heights, but a client
who uses a binary search tree implementation is probably more interested in
understanding the cost in terms of the size. This is a setting in which
composing recurrences does not work as smoothly as we might hope.
Understanding how to mesh them
together, and more generally how to hide analyses that possibly require
more complex types (such as those by
Cutler et al. 2020
)
behind an interface, is ongoing work.

Appendix A
Type preservation for the source language

Type preservation depends on the usual substitution lemmas.

Lemma A.1
.

If
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{{e}{(\theta-x)}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
and

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
, then

{\Gamma}\vdash{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v}\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

Lemma A.2
.

If
{y\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
and
\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
,
then
{v^{\prime}}{\{{v}/{y}\}}
is a value and

\vdash{{v^{\prime}}{\{{v}/{y}\}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

We now have the type preservation theorem.

Theorem

(Type preservation, Theorem
1
)
.

(1)

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{e}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
and
{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}
,
then
\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

(2)

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}

and

{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v}
, then

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}
.

(3)

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}
and

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v}
, then

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}
.

Proof A.3
.

The proof is a simultaneous induction on the height of the
derivation that referred to in each part.
We give just a few of the more interesting cases, starting with part (1).

Case:
{{x}{\theta}}\downarrow{\theta(x)}
.

By the hypothesis,
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{x}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, so by the typing
rules for closures, there must be some
\Gamma^{\prime}
such that

\Gamma^{\prime}(x)=\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\rho
and

\sigma={\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}
, and

\theta
is a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-environment. But that means that in particular,

\vdash{\theta(x)}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}
, as
required.

Case:
{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}
.

The typing must have the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma_{0}}+{\sigma_{1}}}

\{\mbox{${\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\sigma_{i}}\vdash{e_{i}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}$}\}_{{i=0,1}}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

and the evaluation must have the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow{\iota_{i}\,(v_{i})}

{{e_{i}}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{i}}\}}}}\downarrow{v}

{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}

By definition
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{e}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma_{0}}+{\sigma_{1}}}
,
so by the induction hypothesis,

\vdash{\iota_{i}\,v_{i}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma_{0}}+{\sigma_{1}}}
, and hence by
inversion,
\vdash{v_{i}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma_{i}}
. That means that

{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{i}}\}}
is a
(\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\sigma_{i})
-environment, and
hence
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{e_{i}}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{i}}\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
. So by
the induction hypothesis,
\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, as required.

Case:
{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}
.

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\to{\sigma^{\prime}}}
, then
we must show that
{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\to{\sigma^{\prime}}}

as a value. For this we must show that

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\to{\sigma^{\prime}}}
as a closure,
which is precisely the hypothesis we started with.

Case:
{{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}
.

The typing has the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e_{0}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{\sigma}}

{\Gamma}\vdash{e_{1}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}

{\Gamma}\vdash{{e_{0}}\,{e_{1}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

and the evaluation has the form

{{e_{0}}{\theta}}\downarrow{{(\lambda{x}.{e_{0}^{\prime}})}{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}}

{{e_{1}}{\theta}}\downarrow{v_{1}}

{{e_{0}^{\prime}}{{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\downarrow{v}

{{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}

Since
\theta
is a
\Gamma
-environment,

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{e_{0}}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{\sigma}}
, so by the induction
hypothesis,

\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{e_{0}^{\prime}})}{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{\sigma}}
and similarly

\vdash{v_{1}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
. By definition we have that there is some
\Gamma^{\prime}

such that
{\Gamma^{\prime}}\vdash{\lambda{x}.{e_{0}^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{\sigma}}
and

\theta_{0}^{\prime}
is a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-environment; by inversion we have that

{\Gamma^{\prime},x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{e_{0}^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.
Since
\vdash{v_{1}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
,

{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}
is a

(\Gamma^{\prime},x\mathbin{:}\rho)
-environment, and so

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{e_{0}}{{\theta_{0}^{\prime}}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, and so by
the induction hypothesis,

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, as required.

Case:
{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}
.

The typing must have the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

and the evaluation must have the form

{{e^{\prime}}{\theta}}\downarrow{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow{v}

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}

where without loss of generality
we assume
y\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\Gamma
and
y\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\theta
.
By the assumptions and induction hypothesis,

\vdash{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}
, and so by
inversion,
\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\delta}]}
.
From
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

we conclude that

{\Gamma,y\mathbin{:}\delta}\vdash{\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}
.
Since
\theta
is a
\Gamma
-environment, we conclude that

{y\mathbin{:}\delta}\vdash{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}
.
These two judgments allow us to conclude that

{\underline{~}}\vdash{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}
,
so by the induction hypothesis applied to the evaluation of the

\mathtt{mapv}
expression,

\vdash{v^{\prime\prime}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}
. That means that

{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}
is a

(\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}])
-environment, and so by
the induction hypothesis applied to the evaluation of

{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}
,

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, as required.

For (2), suppose

{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow{v}
.
The typing must have the form

{y\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\rho}]}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}

and the evaluation the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v^{\prime\prime}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime\prime}}}\downarrow{v}

{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

As in previous cases,
\vdash{v^{\prime\prime}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\rho}]}
, and so

{\underline{~}}\vdash{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\sigma}]}
, and so
the result follows from the induction hypothesis applied to this

\mathtt{mapv}
expression.

We now prove (3).

Case:
{\mathtt{mapv}_{t}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v}}\downarrow{{v^{\prime}}{\{{v}/{y}\}}}
.

From the typing assumption we have that

{y\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
and

\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
, so the result follows from
Lemma
A.2
.

Case:
{\mathtt{mapv}_{{\rho}\to{F}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}}\downarrow{{(\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}})}{\theta}}
.

The typing must have the form

{y\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{F[\rho^{\prime}]}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\lambda{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{F[\rho^{\prime}]}}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{F[\rho^{\prime}]}}

{\underline{~}}\vdash{\mathtt{mapv}_{{\rho}\to{F}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{F[\sigma^{\prime}]}}

Thus we obtain a typing of the value as

{y\mathbin{:}\rho^{\prime}}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{F[\rho^{\prime}]}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{F[\sigma]}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{F[\sigma]}}

\theta
a
\Gamma
-environment

{\underline{~}}\vdash{{(\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}})}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{F[\sigma]}}

Appendix B
Typeability of extracted recurrences

In this appendix we prove that extracted recurrences are typeable.
It is worth remembering that

\langle\!\langle{\rho}\to{\sigma}\rangle\!\rangle={\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\to{\|\sigma\|}
, so
extraction “commutes” with type substitution in the
expected way.

Lemma B.1
.

\langle\!\langle{\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}\rangle\!\rangle={\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}
. Since
shape functors are a subset of types, this implies that

\langle\!\langle{F}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}\rangle\!\rangle={\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}
and

\langle\!\langle{F}[{\rho}]\rangle\!\rangle={\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}]

Lemma B.2
.

If
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\tau^{\prime}}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\tau}
and

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\tau^{\prime}}
, then

{\Gamma}\vdash{{e}{\{{e^{\prime}}/{x}\}}}\mathbin{:}{\tau}
.

Lemma B.3
.

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{c}\mathbin{:}{\mathsf{C}}
and

{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
,
then
{\Gamma}\vdash{{c}+_{c}{e}}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
.

Proposition

(Typeability of extracted recurrences,
Prop.
2
)
.

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
is in the core language, then

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
.

Proof B.4
.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
; we
just do a few of the cases, since they are all fairly routine.

Case:
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\rho}\vdash{x}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}
.

\|x\|=(0,x,
) and

\|{\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}\|={\mathsf{C}}\times{\langle\!\langle{\rho}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}\rangle\!\rangle}={\mathsf{C}}\times{{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}
;
the recurrence language typing is

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{0}\mathbin{:}{\mathsf{C}}

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{x}\mathbin{:}{\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{x}\mathbin{:}{{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{(0,x)}\mathbin{:}{{\mathsf{C}}\times{{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}}

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\lambda{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{{\rho}\to{\sigma}}
.

\|{\rho}\to{\sigma}\|={\mathsf{C}}\times{({\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\to{\|\sigma\|})}

and we have
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
, so by the induction
hypothesis,

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
and hence

{\Gamma}\vdash{0}\mathbin{:}{\mathsf{C}}

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle)}.{\|e\|}}\mathbin{:}{{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\to{\|\sigma\|}}

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{(0,\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle)}.{\|e\|})}\mathbin{:}{{\mathsf{C}}\times{({\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\to{\|\sigma\|})}}

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

The typing
derivation has the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

so by the induction hypothesis

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e^{\prime}\|}\mathbin{:}{{\mathsf{C}}\times{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}}

and

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]}}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
.
Writing
(c^{\prime},p^{\prime})
for
\|e^{\prime}\|
, by inversion we have that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{c^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\mathsf{C}}
and

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{p^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}
. We must show that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{{c^{\prime}}+_{c}{\mathsf{fold}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,p^{\prime}\,\mathsf{of}\,{(x:{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]})}.{{1}+_{c}{\|e\|}}}}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
.
This follows directly from the typings given by the induction hypothesis,
making use of the fact that

\langle\!\langle{F}\{{\eta}\}\rangle\!\rangle={\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}{\{{\vec{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}
and
Lemma
B.3
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

The typing derivation has the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\rho}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

\vec{\alpha}\notin\mathrm{ftv}(\Gamma)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

The induction hypothesis tells us that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e^{\prime}\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\rho\|}
,
so if
\|e\|^{\prime}=(c^{\prime},p^{\prime})
, then

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{c^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\mathsf{C}}
and

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{p^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}
.
From the latter we conclude that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}

because
\vec{\alpha}\notin\mathrm{ftv}(\Gamma)
implies that

\vec{\alpha}\notin\mathrm{ftv}(\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle)
.
The induction hypothesis also tells us that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}
.
Together with Lemma
B.2
we conclude that

{\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{{\|e\|}{\{{\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}}}/{x}\}}}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}

and so Lemma
B.3
yields the desired conclusion.

Appendix C
The syntactic bounding theorem

In this appendix we prove the syntactic bounding theorem
(Theorem
4
).
The proof relies on two lemmas that describe
bounding for
\mathtt{mapv}
and
\mathtt{fold}
expressions.

Lemma C.1

(Syntactic bounding for
\mathtt{mapv}
)
.

Suppose
\mathrm{ftv}(F)\subseteq\{t\}
and that the following all hold:

(1)

{y\mathbin{:}\rho}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
and

{y\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{E^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle\sigma\rangle\!\rangle}
.

(2)

{\underline{~}}\vdash{v}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\rho}]}
and

{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${v}\preceq_{{F}[{\rho}]}^{\mathrm{val}}{E}$}}
;

(3)

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{w_{0}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
and

{\mathcal{E}_{0}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${w_{0}}\preceq_{\rho}^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{0}}$}}
is a subderivation
of
\mathcal{E}
then

{{v^{\prime}}{\{{w_{0}}/{y}\}}}\preceq_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{val}}{{E^{\prime}}{\{{E_{0}}/{y}\}}}
;

(4)

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}
.

Then

{v^{\prime\prime}}\preceq_{{F}[{\sigma}]}^{\mathrm{val}}{\mbox{${({\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle})}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{E}]$}}
.

Proof C.2
.

The proof is by induction on
F
.

Case:
F=t
.

Assumption (4) tells us that

v^{\prime\prime}={v^{\prime}}{\{{v}/{y}\}}
, so
we must show that
{{v^{\prime}}{\{{v}/{y}\}}}\preceq_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{val}}{{E^{\prime}}{\{{E}/{y}\}}}
,
which follows from assumption (3), taking
v_{0}
and
E_{0}
to be
v
and
E
,
respectively.

Case:
F=\tau_{0}
.

Assumption (4) tells us that

v^{\prime\prime}=v
, so we must show that

{v}\preceq_{\tau_{0}}^{\mathrm{val}}{E}
, which follows from assumption (2).

Case:
F={F_{0}}\times{F_{1}}
.

Assumption (2) and inversion tells us that
v=(v_{0},v_{1})
, and
assumption (4) tells us that
v^{\prime\prime}=(v_{0}^{\prime\prime},v_{1}^{\prime\prime})
, where

\{\mbox{${\mathtt{mapv}_{F_{i}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v_{i}}}\downarrow{v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}$}\}_{{i=0,1}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{{F_{0}}\times{F_{1}}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{(v_{0},v_{1})}}\downarrow{(v_{0}^{\prime\prime},v_{1}^{\prime\prime})}

We must show that

{(v_{0}^{\prime\prime},v_{1}^{\prime\prime})}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{0}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{\pi_{0}\,E}]$},\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{\pi_{1}\,E}]$})}
,
for which it suffices to show that

{v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{\pi_{i}\,E}]$}}

for
i=0,1
.
To do so we apply the induction hypothesis taking
F_{i}
for
F
,

v_{i}
for
v
,
\pi_{i}\,E
for
E
, and
v_{i}^{\prime\prime}
for
v^{\prime\prime}
.
Verifying the assumptions is straightforward, noting that
(3) follows because the derivation that

{v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\pi_{i}\,E}
is a subderivation of

{(v_{0},v_{1})}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}
.

Case:
F={F_{0}}+{F_{1}}
.

Assumption (2) and inversion tells us that

v=\iota_{i}\,v_{i}
, where there is
E_{i}
such that

{v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{i}}
and

\iota_{i}\,E_{i}\leq_{{\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[{\rho}]}E
.
Assumption (4) tells us that
v^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{i}\,v_{i}^{\prime\prime}
, where

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F_{i}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v_{i}}}\downarrow{v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{{F_{0}}+{F_{1}}}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{\iota_{i}\,v_{i}}}\downarrow{\iota_{i}\,v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}

We must show that

\mbox{${\iota_{i}\,v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\mathsf{case}\,E\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}])}.{\iota_{i}\,(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{x}]$}}\}_{i=0,1}}$}.

Let us write
E^{*}
for the right-hand side.
Now we must show that there is
E_{i}^{\prime\prime}
such that

{v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{i}^{\prime\prime}}
and
\iota_{i}\,E_{i}^{\prime\prime}\leq E^{*}
.
We apply the induction hypothesis taking
F_{i}
for
F
,

v_{i}
for
v
,
E_{i}
for
E
, and
v_{i}^{\prime\prime}
for
v^{\prime\prime}
to conclude
that
{v_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{i}^{\prime\prime}}
where

E_{i}^{\prime\prime}=\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{E_{i}}]$}
, and we
notice that

\displaystyle\iota_{i}\,E_{i}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\iota_{i}\,(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{E_{i}}]$})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathsf{case}\,\iota_{i}\,E_{i}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}])}.{\iota_{i}\,(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{i}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{x}]$}}\}_{i=0,1}}

\displaystyle\leq E^{*}

as required. The assumptions for the induction hypothesis are straightforward
to verify, noting that (3) follows because the derivation that

{v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{i}}
is a subderivation of

{\iota_{i}\,v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}
.

Case:
F={\tau_{0}}\to{F_{0}}
.

Assumption (2) and inversion tells us that
v={(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}
, and
assumption (4) tells us that

v^{\prime\prime}={(\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}})}{\theta}
.
We must show that

\displaystyle{(\lambda{x}.{\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e}})}{\theta}

\displaystyle\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}\mbox{${\langle\!\langle{\tau_{0}}\to{F_{0}}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{E}]$}

\displaystyle=\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\tau_{0}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{({({E}\,{x})}_{c},\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F_{0}\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{{({E}\,{x})}_{p}}]$})}.

To do so, fix
{v_{1}}\preceq_{\tau_{0}}^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{1}}
; it suffices to show that

{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e})}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\preceq_{{F}[{\rho}]}{({({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{c},\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}]$})}
.
The evaluation of the left-hand side has the form

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{w^{\prime}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{w^{\prime}}}\downarrow{w}

{{(\mathtt{map}_{F}\,{y}.{v^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{e})}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{w}

so by Lemma
5.4
it suffices to show that

n\leq{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{c}
and

{w}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}]$}}
.
Recalling that
v={(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}
and

{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}
by assumption (2), we have that

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\preceq{{E}\,{E_{1}}}
, and hence

n\leq{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{c}
(our first obligation) and

{w^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}
.
To show that

{w}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\rho\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{\prime}},{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}]$}}
we
apply the induction hypothesis taking
F_{0}
for
F
,
w^{\prime}
for
v
,

{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}
for
E
, and
w
for
v^{\prime\prime}
. Assumptions (1),
(2), and (4) are straightforward to verify. For assumption (3), suppose that

{\underline{~}}\vdash{w_{0}}\mathbin{:}{\rho}
and

{\mathcal{E}_{0}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${w_{0}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{0}}$}}
is a subderivation of

{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${w^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}$}}
.
We need to show that

{{v^{\prime}}{\{{w_{0}}/{y}\}}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{E^{\prime}}{\{{E_{0}}/{y}\}}}
. To do so,
it suffices to show that
\mathcal{E}_{0}
is a subderivation of

{\mathcal{E}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}$}}
, and for this it suffices
to show that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is a subderivation of
\mathcal{E}
.
This follows from examining
\mathcal{E}
:

\dotsb

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{w^{\prime}}

n\leq{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{c}

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

{w^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{({E}\,{E_{1}})}_{p}}

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}}\preceq{{E}\,{E_{1}}}

\dotsb

{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}

Lemma C.3

(Syntactic bounding for
\mathtt{fold}
)
.

Suppose the following all hold:

(1)

{(\mbox{${\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}$})}\preceq_{\sigma}{(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle,x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{E}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}$}}
;

(2)

{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma-x}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
(w.l.o.g.,
x\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\Theta
);

(3)

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime}}
.

Then

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{v^{\prime}}\}}}}\preceq_{\sigma}{\mathsf{fold}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,E^{\prime}\,\mathsf{of}\,{(x:{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]})}.{{1}+_{c}{E\{{\Theta}\}}}}
.

Proof C.4
.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of assumption (3), which
necessarily ends with the rule

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{F,\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime\prime}}

{{\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}}\,{E^{\prime\prime}}}\,{\leq}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}E^{\prime}

{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}\preceq_{\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime}}

To reduce notational clutter, we will write

E^{*}[z]
for

{\mathsf{fold}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,z\,\mathsf{of}\,{(x:{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]})}.{{1}+_{c}{E\{{\Theta}\}}}}
,
so we must show that

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}^{\prime}}\}}}}\preceq{E^{*}[E^{\prime}]}
.
Using the axioms for
\leq
, we have that

E^{*}[E^{\prime}]\geq E^{*}[\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{E^{\prime\prime}}]\geq{1}+_{c}{{E\{{\Theta}\}}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}
.
The evaluation of interest has the form

{{y}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}\}}}}\downarrow^{0}{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}^{\prime}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}^{\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n+1}{v}

We apply Lemma
C.1

by taking
F
for
F
,

\delta
for
\rho
,

{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}
for
v^{\prime}
,

E^{*}[y]
for
E^{\prime}
,
v^{\prime}
for
v
,
E^{\prime\prime}
for
E
, and
v^{\prime\prime}
for
v^{\prime\prime}

(we verify the assumptions momentarily)
to conclude that

{v^{\prime\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}
,
so by (2),

{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{{\Theta}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}

and so by (1),

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}}\preceq{E\{{{\Theta}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}\}}
.
This tells us that

\displaystyle 1+n

\displaystyle\leq 1+{(E\{{{\Theta}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}\})}_{c}

\displaystyle={({1}+_{c}{E\{{{\Theta}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}\}})}_{c}

\displaystyle\leq{(E^{*}[E^{\prime}])}_{c}

and

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{(E\{{{\Theta}{\{{\mbox{${\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[({y}:{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}).{E^{*}[y]},{E^{\prime\prime}}]$}}/{x}\}}}\})}_{p}

\displaystyle\leq{(E^{*}[E^{\prime}])}_{p}

as needed.

We just need to verify the assumptions of Lemma
C.1
:

(1)

{y\mathbin{:}\delta}\vdash{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}
and

{y\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{E^{*}[y]}\mathbin{:}{\langle\!\langle{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

(2)

{\underline{~}}\vdash{v^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\delta}]}
and

{v^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime\prime}}
with derivation
\mathcal{E}
.

(3)

If
{\underline{~}}\vdash{w_{0}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}
and

{\mathcal{E}_{0}}\mathrel{::}{\mbox{${w_{0}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E_{0}}$}}
is a subderivation
of
\mathcal{E}
, then

{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{w_{0}}\}}}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{E^{*}}{\{{E_{0}}/{y}\}}}
.

(4)

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}

(1), (2), and (4) are immediate. Under the assumptions of (3), we
must show that

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{{\theta}{\{{y}\mapsto{w_{0}}\}}}}\preceq{{E^{*}}{\{{E_{0}}/{y}\}}}
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{0}
is a subderivation
of
\mathcal{E}
, the main induction hypothesis applies.

Theorem

(Syntactic bounding theorem, Thm.
4
)
.

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
is in the core language, then

{(\mbox{${\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}$})}\preceq_{\sigma}{(\mbox{${\langle\!\langle\Gamma\rangle\!\rangle}\vdash{\|e\|}\mathbin{:}{\|\sigma\|}$})}
.

Proof C.5
.

The proof is by induction on
{\Gamma}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\tau}
.
Most cases proceed by showing that
{e}\preceq{(c,p)}
for some
c

and
p
, where

{e}\downarrow^{n}{v}
. By
(
\beta_{\mathord{\times}}
)
,
c\leq{(c,p)}_{c}
and

p\leq{(c,p)}_{p}
, so it suffices to show that

n\leq c
and
{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p}
,
and we take advantage of this fact silently.

Case:
{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\sigma}\vdash{x}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\{{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}}\}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\sigma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
;
we must show that

{x}{\theta}\preceq_{{\sigma}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}(0,x)\{{\Theta}\}={(0,\Theta(x))}
.
The evaluation
of
{x}{\theta}
has the form

{{x}{\theta}}\downarrow^{0}{\theta(x)}

The cost bound is immediate. For the value bound we must show that

{\theta(x)}\preceq_{{\sigma}{\{{\vec{\sigma}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta(x)}
.
This follows from the definition of

{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\sigma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{(\,)}\mathbin{:}{\mathtt{unit}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must show that

{(\,)}{\theta}\preceq_{\mathtt{unit}}{(0,(\,))\{{\Theta}\}}=(0,(\,))
. The evaluation of
{(\,)}{\theta}
has the form

{{(\,)}{\theta}}\downarrow^{0}{(\,)}

and we have that
(cost)
0\leq 0
and
(value)
{(\,)}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{(\,)}
by the definition of
\preceq_{\mathtt{unit}}^{\mathrm{val}}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{(e_{0},e_{1})}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma_{0}}\times{\sigma_{1}}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must show that

{{(e_{0},e_{1})}{\theta}}\preceq{(c_{0}+c_{1},(p_{0},p_{1}))\{{\Theta}\}}=((c_{0}+c_{1})\{{\Theta}\},(p_{0},p_{1})\{{\Theta}\})
,
where
\|e_{i}\|=(c_{i},p_{i})
. The evaluation of

{(e_{0},e_{1})}{\theta}
has the form

{{e_{0}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{0}}{v_{0}}

{{e_{1}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{1}}{v_{1}}

{{(e_{0},e_{1})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{0}+n_{1}}{(v_{0},v_{1})}

Cost:

n_{i}\leq c_{i}\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH so

n_{0}+n_{1}\leq c_{0}\{{\Theta}\}+c_{1}\{{\Theta}\}=(c_{0}+c_{1})\{{\Theta}\}
.

Value:

{v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p_{i}\{{\Theta}\}}
by the IH so

{(v_{0},v_{1})}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{(p_{0}\{{\Theta}\},p_{1}\{{\Theta}\})}=(p_{0},p_{1})\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\pi_{i}\,e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma_{i}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must show that

{{(\pi_{i}\,e)}{\theta}}\preceq(c,\pi_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\}=(c\{{\Theta}\},(\pi_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\})
, where

{\|e\|}=(c,p)
.
The evaluation of
{(\pi_{i}\,e)}{\theta}

has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{(v_{0},v_{1})}

{{(\pi_{i}\,e)}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v_{i}}

Cost:

n\leq(c)\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH.

Value:

{(v_{0},v_{1})}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}

by the IH, so

{v_{i}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\pi_{i}\,(p\{{\Theta}\})}=(\pi_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\}

by the definition of

\preceq_{{\sigma_{0}}\times{\sigma_{1}}}^{\mathrm{val}}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\iota_{i}\,e}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma_{0}}+{\sigma_{1}}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must show that

{{(\iota_{i}\,e)}{\theta}}\preceq(c,\iota_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\}=(c\{{\Theta}\},(\iota_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\})
, where

{\|e\|}=(c,p)
.
The evaluation of
{(\iota_{i}\,e)}{\theta}

has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\iota_{i}\,e)}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{\iota_{i}\,v}

Cost:

n\leq c\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH.

Value:

{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
by the IH, and

\iota_{i}\,(p\{{\Theta}\})\leq\iota_{i}\,(p\{{\Theta}\})
,
so

{\iota_{i}\,v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\iota_{i}\,(p\{{\Theta}\})}=(\iota_{i}\,p)\{{\Theta}\}
by the definition of

\preceq_{{\sigma_{0}}+{\sigma_{1}}}^{\mathrm{val}}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must show that

{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}\preceq({c}+_{c}{\mathsf{case}\,p\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})}\}_{i=0,1}})\{{\Theta}\}={{c\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{\mathsf{case}\,p\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}}}
,
where

{\|e\|}=(c,p)
and

\|e_{i}\|=(c_{i},p_{i})
.
The evaluation of

{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}

has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{\iota_{i}\,v}

{{e_{i}}{\theta\{{x}\mapsto{v}\}}}\downarrow^{n_{i}}{v_{i}}

{{(\mathtt{case}\,e\,\mathtt{of}\,\{{x}.{e}_{i}\}_{{i=0,1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n+n_{i}}{v_{i}}

By the IH for
e
,
{\iota_{i}\,v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
,
so there is
some
E^{\prime}
such that

\mbox{${v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E}$}^{\prime}

and

\iota_{i}\,E^{\prime}\leq p\{{\Theta}\}
.
If we set
\theta^{\prime}={\theta}{\{{v}\mapsto{x}\}}
and

\Theta^{\prime}={\Theta}{\{{E^{\prime}}/{x}\}}
, then

{\theta^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta^{\prime}}
, so by the IH for
e_{i}
,

{{e_{i}}{\theta^{\prime}}}\preceq{(c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})}
.
Since
\iota_{i}\,E^{\prime}\leq p\{{\Theta}\}
, we have

\displaystyle(c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle=(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{case}\,\iota_{i}\,E^{\prime}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{case}\,p\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}

and so

\displaystyle(c\{{\Theta}\}+c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})

\displaystyle={c\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{(c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})}

\displaystyle\leq{c\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{\mathsf{case}\,p\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}}

which we use to complete the next set of calculations.

Cost:

n\leq c\{{\Theta}\}
and

n_{i}\leq c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\}
, so

\displaystyle n+n_{i}

\displaystyle\leq c\{{\Theta}\}+c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle\leq{(c\{{\Theta}\}+c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})}_{c}

\displaystyle\leq{({c\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{\mathsf{case}\,p\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}})}_{c}.

Value:

\displaystyle v_{i}

\displaystyle\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle\leq{(c\{{\Theta}\}+c_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\},p_{i}\{{\Theta^{\prime}}\})}_{p}

\displaystyle\leq{({c\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{\mathsf{case}\,p\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{(c_{i},p_{i})\{{\Theta-x}\}}\}_{i=0,1}})}_{p}.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\lambda{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma^{\prime}}\to{\sigma}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
; we must
show that

{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\preceq{(0,\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{\|e\|})\{{\Theta}\}}=(0,\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{\|e\|\{{\Theta-x}\}})
.
The evaluation of
{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}
has the form

{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{0}{{(\lambda{x}.{e})}{\theta}}

so the cost claim is immediate.

Value:

Fix any
{v^{\prime}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime}}
. We must show that

{{e}{\theta\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime}}\}}}\preceq{(\lambda{(x\mathbin{:}\langle\!\langle\sigma^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle)}.{\|e\|\{{\Theta-x}\}}){E^{\prime}}}
;
by definition,
(
\beta_{\rightarrow}
)
, and Weakening, it suffices to show
that

{e\theta\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime}}\}}\preceq{\|e\|\{{{\Theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{E^{\prime}}\}}}\}}
.
Since
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
and
{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{E^{\prime}}
, this follows from
the induction hypothesis.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{{e_{0}}\,{e_{1}}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
. We must show that

{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}\preceq({(c_{0}+c_{1})}+_{c}{{p_{0}}\,{p_{1}}})\{{\Theta}\}
,
where

{\|e_{i}\|}=(c_{i},p_{i})
.
The evaluation of
{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}
has the form

{{e_{0}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{0}}{{(\lambda{x}.{e_{0}^{\prime}})}{\theta^{\prime}}}

{{e_{1}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{1}}{v_{1}}

{{e_{0}^{\prime}}{\theta^{\prime}\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{({e_{0}}\,{e_{1}})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n_{0}+n_{1}+n}{v}

By the IH,
n_{0}\leq c_{0}\{{\Theta}\}
,

{{(\lambda{x}.{e_{0}^{\prime}})}{\theta^{\prime}}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p_{0}\{{\Theta}\}}
,

n_{1}\leq c_{1}\{{\Theta}\}
, and

{v_{1}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p_{1}\{{\Theta}\}}
. By definition of
\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}
,

{{e_{0}^{\prime}}{\theta^{\prime}\{{x}\mapsto{v_{1}}\}}}\preceq{(p_{0}\{{\Theta}\}){(p_{1}\{{\Theta}\})}}=({p_{0}}\,{p_{1}})\{{\Theta}\}
,
so
n\leq{(({p_{0}}\,{p_{1}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{c}
and

{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{(({p_{0}}\,{p_{1}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{p}}
.

Cost:

n_{0}+n_{1}+n\leq c_{0}\{{\Theta}\}+c_{1}\{{\Theta}\}+{(({p_{0}}\,{p_{1}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{c}\leq{(({(c_{0}+c_{1})}+_{c}{{p_{0}}\,{p_{1}}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{c}
.

Value:

v\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{(({p_{0}}\,{p_{1}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{p}}\leq{(({(c_{0}+c_{1})}+_{c}{{p_{0}}\,{p_{1}}})\{{\Theta}\})}_{p}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{delay}\,e}\mathbin{:}{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
. We must show that

{(\mathtt{delay}\,e)}{\theta}\preceq(0,\|e\|)\{{\Theta}\}=(0,\|e\|\{{\Theta}\})
.
The evaluation of
{(\mathtt{delay}\,e)}{\theta}
has the form

{{(\mathtt{delay}\,e)}{\theta}}\downarrow^{0}{{(\mathtt{delay}\,e)}{\theta}}

so (cost)
0\leq 0
and (value) since

{{e}{\theta}}\preceq{\|e\|\{{\Theta}\}}
by the IH,

{{(\mathtt{delay}\,e)}{\theta}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\|e\|\{{\Theta}\}}

by the definition of
\preceq_{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}^{\mathrm{val}}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{force}\,e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
. We must show that

{(\mathtt{force}\,e)}{\theta}\preceq({c}+_{c}{p})\{{\Theta}\}
,
where
{\|e\|}=(c,p)
. The
evaluation of
{(\mathtt{force}\,e)}{\theta}
has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{{(\mathtt{delay}\,e^{\prime})}{\theta^{\prime}}}

{{e^{\prime}}{\theta^{\prime}}}\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}{v}

{{(\mathtt{force}\,e)}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n+n^{\prime}}{v}

By the IH,
n\leq c\{{\Theta}\}
and

{{(\mathtt{delay}\,e^{\prime})}{\theta^{\prime}}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
, so by definition
of
\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}
,
{{e^{\prime}}{\theta^{\prime}}}\preceq{p\{{\Theta}\}}
and hence

n^{\prime}\leq{(p\{{\Theta}\})}_{c}
and

{v}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{(p\{{\Theta}\})}_{p}}
.
So (cost)

n+n^{\prime}\leq c\{{\Theta}\}+{p\{{\Theta}\}}_{c}\leq{(({c}+_{c}{p})\{{\Theta}\})}_{c}
and
(value)

v\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{{p\{{\Theta}\}}_{p}}\leq{(({c}+_{c}{p})\{{\Theta}\})}_{p}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
. We must show that

{(\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}\preceq(c,\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{p})\{{\Theta}\}
,
where

{\|e\|}=(c,p)
. The evaluation of

{(\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}
has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}}

Cost:

n\leq c\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH.

Value:

By the IH we have that

{v}\preceq_{\langle\!\langle{F}[{\delta}]\rangle\!\rangle}^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
, and
so by Lemma
5.1
,

{v}\preceq_{F,\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
.
Since

\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{p}\leq\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{p}
, the value bound
follows by definition of
\preceq_{\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{d}_{\delta}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{{F}[{\delta}]}
.

Fix
{\theta}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
. We must show that

{(\mathtt{d}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}\preceq(c,\mathsf{d}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{p})\{{\Theta}\}=(c\{{\Theta}\},\mathsf{d}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{(p\{{\Theta}\})})
,
where

{\|e\|}=(c,p)
. The evaluation of

{(\mathtt{d}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}
has the form

{{e}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}}

{{(\mathtt{d}_{\delta}\,{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

Cost:

n\leq c\{{\Theta}\}
by the IH.

Value:

By the IH,
{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p\{{\Theta}\}}
, and so by
definition of
\preceq_{\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}
,
there is
E
such that

{v}\preceq_{F,\delta}^{\mathrm{val}}{E}
and

\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{E}\leq p\{{\Theta}\}
.
This latter fact along with the axioms for
\leq
tell us that

E\leq\mathsf{d}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{(}\mathsf{c}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{E})\leq\mathsf{d}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,{(}p\{{\Theta}\})
.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

The type derivation has the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\delta}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{F}[{{\sigma}\,\mathtt{susp}}]}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

Fix

{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
and without loss of generality assume
that
x\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\Gamma\cup\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\theta\cup\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}\nolimits\Theta
; we must show that

{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}\preceq{c^{\prime}}+_{c}{\mathsf{fold}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,p^{\prime}\,\mathsf{of}\,{(x:{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]})}.{{1}+_{c}{\|e\|}}}

where
\|e^{\prime}\|=(c^{\prime},p^{\prime})
.
The evaluation of
{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}
has the form

{{e^{\prime}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,e^{\prime}\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n^{\prime}+n+1}{v}

and so the following is also an evaluation, where we write

\theta^{\prime}
for
{\theta}{\{{z}\mapsto{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}\}}
:

{{z}{\theta^{\prime}}}\downarrow^{0}{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}

{\mathtt{mapv}_{F}\,{y}.{{(\mathtt{delay}\,(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,y\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e}))}{\theta^{\prime}}}\,\mathtt{into}\,{v^{\prime}}}\downarrow{v^{\prime\prime}}

{{e}{{\theta^{\prime}}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,z\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta^{\prime}}}\downarrow^{n+1}{v}

The IH for
e^{\prime}
tells us that

n^{\prime}\leq c^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}
and

{\mathtt{c}_{\delta}\,{v^{\prime}}}\preceq^{\mathrm{val}}{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}
; combined with the
IH for
e
, Lemma
C.3
tells us that

{{(\mathtt{fold}_{\delta}\,z\,\mathtt{of}\,{x}.{e})}{\theta^{\prime}}}\preceq{\mathsf{fold}_{\langle\!\langle\delta\rangle\!\rangle}\,p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}\,\mathsf{of}\,{(x:{{\langle\!\langle F\rangle\!\rangle}[{\|\sigma\|}]})}.{{1}+_{c}{\|e\|\{{\Theta}\}}}}

as required.

Case:
{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}
.

The type derivation has the form

{\Gamma}\vdash{e^{\prime}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma^{\prime}}

{\Gamma,x\mathbin{:}{\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\sigma^{\prime}}}\vdash{e}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

\vec{\alpha}
not free in any
\Gamma(y)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e}}\mathbin{:}{\sigma}

Fix
{\theta}\preceq_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Theta}
and without loss of generality
assume
x
is fresh for
\Gamma
,
\theta
, and
\Theta
and that
no
\alpha_{i}
is free in any
\Theta(y)
. We must show that

{(\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e})}{\theta}\preceq({c^{\prime}}+_{c}{{\|e\|}{\{{\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}}}/{x}\}}})\{{\Theta}\}={c^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}+_{c}{{\|e\|\{{\Theta}\}}{\{{\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}}/{x}\}}}

where
\|e^{\prime}\|=(c^{\prime},p^{\prime})
.
The evaluation has the form

{{e^{\prime}}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n^{\prime}}{v^{\prime}}

{{e}{{\theta}{\{{x}\mapsto{v^{\prime}}\}}}}\downarrow^{n}{v}

{{(\mathtt{let}\,{x}={e^{\prime}}\,\mathtt{in}\,{e})}{\theta}}\downarrow^{n^{\prime}+n}{v}

The IH for
e^{\prime}
tells us that
n^{\prime}\leq c^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}
and

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{val}}{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}
. If we can show that

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{\forall{\vec{\alpha}}.\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{val}}{\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}}
,
then the induction hypothesis applied to
e
provides the remaining
pieces of the argument.
For this we need to show that for any closed
\vec{\rho}
,

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{{\sigma^{\prime}}{\{{\vec{\rho}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}^{\mathrm{val}}{{(\Lambda{\vec{\alpha}}.{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}})}\,{\langle\!\langle\vec{\rho}\rangle\!\rangle}}
,
and by
(
\beta_{\forall}
)
and weakening, it suffices to show

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{{\sigma^{\prime}}{\{{\vec{\rho}}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}^{\mathrm{val}}{{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}{\{{\langle\!\langle\vec{\rho}\rangle\!\rangle}/{\vec{\alpha}}\}}}
.
This in turn requires us to show that if

\mathrm{ftv}(\sigma)=\{\vec{\alpha},\vec{\beta}\}
, then for any
closed
\vec{\rho^{\prime}}
,

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{{\sigma^{\prime}}{\{{\vec{\rho},\vec{\rho^{\prime}}}/{\vec{\alpha},\vec{\beta}}\}}}^{\mathrm{val}}{{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}{\{{\langle\!\langle\vec{\rho},\vec{\rho^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle}/{\vec{\alpha},\vec{\beta}}\}}}
,
which follows from the fact that

{v^{\prime}}\preceq_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{val}}{p^{\prime}\{{\Theta}\}}
.
